# Product backlog

This is the implementation tracking source of truth. The execution strategy and
definition of done live in [`README.md`](./README.md).

## Status and priority

- `done` — completed and validated; Evidence contains a commit.
- `in progress` — the single item currently being implemented.
- `next` — first item to pull after the current checkpoint.
- `ready` — sufficiently defined and waiting on its dependencies.
- `blocked` — cannot proceed until the named dependency or approval is resolved.
- `later` — intentionally ordered behind higher-priority work.
- `deferred` — outside the initial release.

Priorities describe product value, not strict technical order:

- `P0` — required for the mobile-first release.
- `P1` — backend/reliability work or mobile completeness needed before production.
- `P2` — admin-web delivery, the lowest-priority application surface.
- `P3` — explicitly deferred work.

Decisions taken under the blanket approval: Assistants and Administrators read any Client (Clients have no Branch) and act on Bookings/Waitlist only at assigned Branches (the list flags `actionable`); a pending-activation Client may receive cash Payments and Bookings, so an invited Client with a Subscription, Payment, or Booking cannot be cancelled (`409 invitation_has_activity`) and is skipped by the 30-day expiry cleanup; search is a bounded 20-row case-insensitive contains on name/email/phone (min 2 chars, no index yet); resend throttling answers `429 email_throttled` without a retry time; history stays an unpaginated array like the Client endpoint. Smoke-tested against the local API as camila (search, overview, quotes, invite → cash → 409 cancel → 429 resend → fresh invite cancelled → 404; Trainer 403). Decisions taken under the blanket approval: block end may only be set or moved earlier (R1); snake_case codes, reusing Booking's `no_eligible_subscription`; reconcilers are worker interval sweeps like the renewal reminder (pg-boss `schedule: false`), no new queues; an end with the same resolved date is an equivalent retry, a later one `409 availability_block_ended`, an earlier one shortens; ending needs only ownership, create/replace re-check the Branch assignment and archival; a Client without an Account gets no reminder but the marker settles it; staff notices open `nutritionist_agenda`, Client ones `evaluation`; lock-screen push text and sound flags live in `evaluation-notifications.ts` for NOT-002; audit correlation id is the Evaluation id; both Client lists use the cursor page shape (20 default, 50 max); Administrator list/cancel routes wait for WEB-008 (the use case already allows Administrators); no Evaluation fixture, so month-rule specs stay deterministic. Gaps: D15 callers arrive with WEB-003/WEB-004; push delivery is NOT-002; Evaluation retention waits for BE-007; the six design follow-ups belong to MOB-016. |

Decisions taken under the blanket approval: Client entry points are the two Perfil rows (the Home quick action is MOB-019); the effective date offers today plus 14 days (the API allows any later date; a longer horizon has no screen); the time grid spans 06:00–22:00; removal always confirms, edits confirm only when bookings exist; the Client subscription gate uses `active`/`expiring_soon` and the server still decides eligibility; the Nutritionist agenda loads 50 entries (no "Ver más" yet). Decided under the blanket approval: where MOB-016 differed from the follow-ups (button labels «Aplicar cambio» / «Quitar horario», removal always confirming, the date-chip strip, the combined «Desde el … · hasta el …» line, the S15 notice body) the design follows the app; checklist 30 §8 now counts seven types on 19 and six on S20. |

Verified 2026-09-22: unit 361/362 (the known pg-boss runtime env failure), e2e 75/83 (the 8 known `app.e2e` role `test` failures), integration 33 suites/136 tests via `TEST_DATABASE_URL`, including 5 new real-PostgreSQL cases (eligible values, ineligible schedules, idempotent rerun that keeps a cancelled occurrence cancelled, horizon advance, Branch and Trainer overlap, concurrent runs), `pnpm check:api` clean, touched files lint clean (the 207 repo lint errors are pre-existing elsewhere); smoke on `pilates_dev_sim`: `node dist/src/worker-main.js` added Thursday 22 Oct's three Sessions (74 → 77, same Branch/Class Type/Trainer/capacity/50 min as 15 Oct), a second run added none, no failure record; `node dist/src/main.js` served them on `GET /sessions` and `/sessions/{id}`, Lucía got `409 no_eligible_subscription` (her Plan ends 12 Oct), Sofía booked (12 → 11 seats) and cancelled (back to 12). Decisions taken under the blanket approval: schedules at an archived Branch, with an archived Class Type, or whose Trainer lacks the Trainer Role or the Branch assignment are skipped (BKM-001 validation); an occurrence that would overlap a scheduled Session is skipped silently and counted, and older schedules (`createdAt`, then `id`) materialize first so the overlap winner is deterministic; duration comes from the Class Type at materialization time and capacity from the schedule; one statement per schedule without an explicit transaction; development fixtures still write Sessions so `db:fixtures` works without a worker and the reconciler only fills the rest. Gaps: schedule edits with an effective date (update/replace/remove unbooked future Sessions) arrive with their commands in ADM-001/WEB-003; an exception that moves a generated Session's start must keep the reconciler from recreating the original start (ADM-001); Class Type or Branch archival does not withdraw Sessions already materialized. |

Verified 2026-09-23: unit 368/369 (the known pg-boss runtime env failure), e2e 75/83 (the 8 known `app.e2e` role `test` failures), integration 34 suites/143 tests via `TEST_DATABASE_URL`, including 7 new real-PostgreSQL cases (zero-Booking cancellation at the exact due instant and not 1 ms before, with Waitlist closure and both notices and a rerun that adds nothing; a booked Session keeps its marker and is never re-checked after dropping to zero; a Session that drops to zero before the check stays bookable and is cancelled at the check; an Administrator-cancelled Session is ignored; `session_started` closure only at start, without a notice, once; a Booking landing while the check holds the Session row commits and the check retries and leaves the Session scheduled, and a mutation to `FOR UPDATE` makes that case fail; two concurrent workers cancel and notify once), `pnpm check:api` clean, touched files lint clean; smoke on `pilates_dev_sim` with three Urubó Sessions for Emma (A in 4 min, capacity 1, booked by Lucía with Valeria's Entry; B in 60 min, empty; C in 115 min, booked by Sofía): `node dist/src/worker-main.js` at start cancelled B (`zero_bookings`, no actor, marker set), set only the marker on A and C, left the 6 already-ended unchecked Sessions alone; `node dist/src/main.js` showed Emma `GET /notifications` "Tu clase de Mat Pilates de hoy 01:00 se canceló: no tuvo reservas." (`trainer_schedule`, unread 1), B `404` on `/sessions/{id}` and `403` on booking, Valeria's Waitlist join inside the window `403`; the minute sweep closed A's Entry as `session_started` at 04:04:41 (A started 04:04:00) with no notice to Valeria; no `*_failed` events. Decisions taken under the blanket approval: sweep every minute like Evaluation reminders (each check at most a minute late), empty check before Waitlist closure; the check stays due until the Session ends so one missed while the worker was down still settles an empty class in progress, while an already-ended Session is left alone; Trainer copy follows design follow-up 15 ("Tu clase de {Class Type} de hoy HH:MM se canceló: no tuvo reservas.", or "del {weekday d de month}, HH:MM" on another Bolivia day), type `session_cancelled`, category `booking_schedule`, destination `trainer_schedule` (Mi agenda) and business key `session-cancelled:{id}` shared with the Administrator cancel so one Session never notifies twice; Clients still on the Waitlist of an automatically cancelled Session get the same notice as for an Administrator cancellation (BOOK-001), and none for `session_started`; no audit record for the automatic cancellation (BOOK-001); Subscription expiry stays derived at purchase time (`isSubscriptionEffective`), so Session-start finalization writes nothing to Subscriptions. Gaps: the Staff app does not route the `trainer_schedule` destination on tap yet (MOB-017 gap); an edit that moves a Session's `starts_at` must clear the marker (ADM-001); push delivery of these notices waits for NOT-002. |

Both Promotions unpublished in the admin web («Dejar de publicar»), `GET /promotions` `[]`, the old public URL `404`; on the iPhone the mounted Home still showed both cards until a pull to refresh removed the section, leaving the plan card under the greeting. Both then deleted through the admin web dialog; rows left in `pilates_dev_sim`: `667ced58…` («Nueva sala de Reformer en Urubó») and `d4e90593…` («Clases de Barre los sábados») archived (10 archived, 0 live), their two image assets released for the worker's next sweep; no `pilates_test_*` created. The 404-between-list-and-image case is covered by the Jest spec only (it cannot be timed by hand). Decisions taken under the blanket approval: React Native's own `Image` and a snapping `ScrollView` suffice (no expo-image, which is not in the app despite the brief, and no carousel library: no dependency commit); the list query keeps Home's `refetchOnMount: "always"` with the app defaults (staleTime 0, gcTime 5 min, refetch on foreground through the focus manager, no retry) and stays out of the ADR-0035 persisted allowlist, matching the API's `private, no-cache` (ADR-0063), while the immutable public images are left to the native image cache; a Promotion whose chosen image fails to load is dropped from the carousel (a card without its image is not a Promotion) and the list is re-read once per failed URL so a re-published image with new URLs comes back; the section hides completely when the list is empty or fails (Home never breaks for the carousel); pull to refresh refreshes every Home query, not only the carousel; the section label is the export's «Novedades del estudio»; the phone card is full width (UPL-002 sized the 960/1440 variants for a 345–400 css px card) and the tablet card stops at 720 pt with the next card peeking; the dots are the export's pill style; no tap target on a card. Reuse decisions: `resolveApiUrl` extracted from the fetcher's `joinApiUrl` at its second user; the source picker is a pure module beside `home-format.ts`; the Staff app's `useIsTablet` was not moved to mobile-core because the card cap is a width rule, not a layout switch; the QR data-URI approach of `payment-screen.tsx` was not reused because the public images are unauthenticated and immutable, so a plain `Image` URL keeps the native cache. | MOB-028   | P2       | Client mobile       | Keep the pending registration across launches (continuation in SecureStore, cleared on verification, cancellation, or lapse) so «Revisa tu correo» replaces the form until the email is verified, as 01 says, and «El registro venció» can actually appear | MOB-021 | Client 01 | done | Found 2026-10-01 while building MOB-021: the pending registration lives only in memory, so the 7-day lapse frame is reachable only if the app stays open for 7 days. The backend keeps resend and correction neutral, so the app infers the lapse from its own 7-day window. **Done 2026-10-01** (red `086443c`, mobile-core `744f1bd`, Client wiring `4307829`, guide `9290742`). The row matched: `AuthenticationFlow` held the pending registration in `useState` only. New `auth/pending-registration-store.ts` (load/save/clear) and `auth/use-pending-registration.ts` (restore at launch, write-through `setPending`); `authentication-flow.tsx` takes an optional `pendingRegistrationStore`, threaded through `MobileRuntime` and `MobileNavigationProps`, and only the Client `App.tsx` creates the native one, so the Staff app and every existing spec are unchanged. **Storage decisions (blanket approval):** SecureStore, not AsyncStorage, because the continuation is a bearer secret for correction, resend, and cancellation; one key `client.pending-registration` beside the credential keys, holding JSON `{ email, pendingExpiresAt, registrationContinuation }` (the device's own 7-day window, restarted by each correction or resend as before); an unreadable value is deleted and a storage error counts as nothing kept, so a locked keychain never blocks the launch or the registration; the flow renders nothing until the load settles (as identity restoring already does) so Login never flashes; a link that opens the app skips the restore and goes to its own screen. **Clearing:** cancellation, «Registrarme de nuevo», a successful verification on this device, and any successful login (a login means there is nothing left to continue; a rejected one keeps it); a lapse is shown once («El registro venció») and cleared in the same load, so the next launch opens Login. Logout does not touch it. Checks: mobile-core Jest 34 suites / 143 (store 4, flow persistence 5), client 110, staff 66, `tsc` in all three packages. Surface: iPad Pro 11 M4 (57B2…) registered `mob028-ipad-…`, `stopApp` + relaunch → «Revisa tu correo» with the address and no Login, «Cancelar registro» → confirm → relaunch → Login; iPhone 17 Pro (7C33…) registered `mob028-iphone-…`, relaunch → «Revisa tu correo», «Volver a iniciar sesión», login as Lucía → Inicio, logout → relaunch → Login. The lapse frame was not reached on a device (it needs 7 real days); the specs cover it. Three pending registrations from the iPhone runs (two flow retries and the passing run) remain in `pilates_dev_sim` until the 7-day cleanup (soft delete only); the iPad one was cancelled. |

Surface: the hand fixes remove unused identifiers and dead code only, but they touch source, so both apps ran on the iPhone 16 Pro (iOS 18.2) and the iPad Pro 11 through Expo Go against the API on `pilates_dev_sim` with fresh fixtures (client Metro on 8083 because another project holds 8081, staff on 8082): Client as Lucía (mobile-core login, Perfil, Editar perfil, Reservar → a Session booked and cancelled through the committed `book-and-cancel-class.yaml`), Staff as Camila (login, Buscar cliente, Lucía's overview) and as Paola (Pagos pendientes root, Revisar pago of Isabel's Bs 560 transfer). Maestro drove both devices at the first attempt (the iPad driver connected with the iPhone shut down); every screen rendered as before, the iPad split view included. The committed `profile-photo.yaml` reached Editar perfil and then failed at the upload because no worker was running to process the image (the API answered 201/204/202 and the app polled `GET /uploads/:id` 42 times), an environment gap, not a change. Pull request https://github.com/DaleneyRuben/emma-pilates/pull/9, opened for the user to merge (merge commit, never squash). CI run [37703313673](https://github.com/DaleneyRuben/emma-pilates/actions/runs/37703313673) green at the first attempt with all three chains, as the `.github/**` touch demands: changes 6 s, quality-api 2 m 4 s, quality-web 1 m 55 s, quality-mobile 1 m 59 s (up from about 2 min of typecheck alone; the three lint and three format steps fit inside the same job time), contracts 1 m 11 s, web 2 m 26 s, mobile 3 m 0 s, api 7 m 2 s. **Done 2026-10-07** (twenty-sixth session). **Decisions taken under the blanket approval**: (1) one shared config in a new development-only `packages/tooling` (`@pilates/tooling`, exports `./eslint/mobile`), the home ADR-0013 reserves for tooling configuration, rather than three copies or a config inside mobile-core, which is a runtime package; the admin web and backend keep their own configs; (2) no `eslint-config-expo`, for the reasons above; (3) the recommended sets without type information, like the admin web (the backend's type-checked variant stays its own); (4) Prettier separate from ESLint as in the admin web, not through `eslint-plugin-prettier` as in the backend, so lint output stays about code and the pass is faster; (5) `printWidth: 100` for the three mobile packages (the admin web and backend stay at 80) because of the file-size rule above; (6) `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` with Airbnb's `{ vars: "all", args: "after-used", ignoreRestSiblings: true }` so props dropped before a `...rest` spread are not errors; (7) `@typescript-eslint/no-require-imports` off in `*.spec.{ts,tsx}` and `jest.setup.ts` only, because `jest.mock` factories are hoisted above the imports; (8) `react-hooks/globals` off in spec files only (test probes); (9) `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` and `react-hooks/purity` downgraded to `warn` for all files because the apps do not enable the React Compiler and the nine sites are pre-existing patterns whose rewrite is a behaviour change (MOB-052); the other React Hooks rules stay errors; (10) the two `exhaustive-deps` warnings left as warnings (adding `onVerified` could re-run a token verification; the other is deliberately once per launch), listed in MOB-052; (11) the one real defect is not fixed here: one inline disable with a comment and the new row MOB-051; (12) the Prettier commit and the hand-fix commits are separate per package, and every hand fix is a removal of an unused identifier or a comment; (13) ESLint stays on 9; (14) the `eslint` globals are browser + node + `__DEV__`, with `jest` added for test files (typescript-eslint turns `no-undef` off for TypeScript, so this only matters for rules that read the list); (15) `format:check` covers `src/**/*.{ts,tsx}` plus `App.tsx`, `index.ts`, and `jest.setup.ts` (the apps) or `jest.setup.ts` (mobile-core), the same shape as the admin web's globs; (16) `packages/tooling/**` added to the mobile paths filter because the mobile packages are its only consumers. Leftovers not taken: the `act()` warnings the two app suites print; `scripts/mobile-release-smoke.mjs` is outside every lint script. |
| MOB-051   | P2       | Client mobile       | Fix the conditional hook in Perfil: `profile-screen.tsx` calls `useClientPhoto` (which wraps TanStack `useQuery`) after its loading and failure returns, so the number of hooks changes when the profile query goes from loading to loaded or failed, which React reports as a change in the order of hooks and can throw «Rendered more hooks than during the previous render»; move the call above the early returns (the hook already accepts `null`) and cover the loading → loaded transition in `profile.spec.tsx`; then drop the `eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/rules-of-hooks` that MOB-050 left on the call | MOB-050 | Client 18 | done | Found by MOB-050's first lint run on 2026-10-07 (`react-hooks/rules-of-hooks`). The Simulator does not show it today because the persisted query cache usually restores the profile before Perfil mounts; a cold cache on a fresh install is the case to reproduce. **Compared with the code 2026-10-07** (twenty-seventh session, branch `fix/mob-051-perfil-conditional-hook`). The row was right about the defect and half right about why it stays hidden. `ProfileScreen` calls seven hooks before any return (`useNavigation`, `useMobileIdentity`, `useClientFetcher`, and the four generated query hooks for the profile, the subscription, the unread count, and the credit), then returns early while `profile.isPending` («Cargando…») and again when `profile.isError` or the response status is not 200 (`QueryFailure` with Reintentar), and only then calls `useClientPhoto`, which itself calls two hooks (`useClientFetcher`, that is `useContext`, and TanStack's `useQuery`, which adds several of its own: a context read, a `useState`, a `useSyncExternalStore`, effects). React 19.2.3 (the `react-native` 0.86.3 Fabric renderer, `ReactFabric-dev.js`) does not merely warn when the count grows between renders: during an update, the first hook call beyond the previous list makes `updateWorkInProgressHook` throw `Error("Rendered more hooks than during the previous render.")`, in development and in release builds alike; the development-only warning «React has detected a change in the order of Hooks called by ProfileScreen» is printed when a hook at a given position changes type, and the shrinking case throws «Rendered fewer hooks than expected». Nothing in the apps catches it (no error boundary in client-mobile or mobile-core), so the thrown error unmounts the root: a red box in Expo Go, a crash in a release build. The transition is reached twice: pending → loaded (a cold cache, Perfil mounted before the profile request answers) and failed → loaded (Reintentar after a failed profile request succeeds, which is the same screen instance going from `isError` to data). Why the Simulator never showed it: besides the persisted cache the row names, the Inicio tab mounts first and calls the same `useGetCurrentClientProfile`, so with the API on localhost the profile is settled milliseconds after login, long before a finger reaches the Perfil tab (React Navigation mounts the tab lazily on its first focus); the eight existing Perfil specs open Perfil through Inicio for the same reason and never render it pending, which is why they are green with the defect in place. The Design column said Client 10 (Pago); Perfil is screen 18, corrected here. No other screen calls a hook after an early return: lint's `rules-of-hooks` reports only this site, and a scan of the 113 hand-written `.tsx` files of client-mobile, staff-mobile, and mobile-core for a top-level hook call after a top-level `return` found the same single site (Staff Perfil's `account-avatar.tsx` and every other data screen call their hooks before branching). **Built 2026-10-07** (twenty-seventh session): red `16970245` (two specs in `profile-screen.spec.tsx`: one holds the `GET /clients/current` request behind a gate so Perfil mounts on «Cargando perfil» and then releases it, one answers 500 and then lets Reintentar succeed; both failed with «Rendered more hooks than during the previous render» thrown from `useClientPhoto`'s `useQuery`), green `aaa32d8e` (the `useClientPhoto` call moved up with the other seven hooks, fed `profile.data.data.photo?.imageAssetId ?? null` when the response is 200 and `null` otherwise, so its query stays disabled until the profile is loaded; the inline `eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/rules-of-hooks` and its comment went with the line they annotated; `use-client-photo.ts` untouched), `62d80617` (the gate's `release` typed `() => void`, found by typecheck), `e73efa8c` (at the user's request mid-session: `my-bookings.spec.tsx` and `reschedule-card.spec.tsx` keyed their stubbed `GET /sessions?…from…until` route by today's civil date while the screen asks for the Booking's own day, which is tomorrow's within an hour of midnight in Bolivia, so both failed at 23:13; they now derive the range from the Booking's `startsAt`), `8b5fd341` (the retry spec's 500 keeps failing until the spec releases it, because Inicio and Perfil both mount the profile query with `refetchOnMount: "always"` and a single failure was overtaken by the second mount's refetch once in the full run). No refactor commit: nothing left to tidy once the call moved. Verified from a clean shell (`env -u DATABASE_URL -u NODE_OPTIONS -u TEST_DATABASE_URL`, Node 24.13.0, pnpm 11.19.0): client-mobile lint exit 0 with 0 errors and the same 3 React Compiler warnings as before (no inline disable left in the three mobile packages), `format:check` clean, typecheck clean, Jest `--ci --forceExit` 34 suites / 140 tests (138 + the two new specs) green in two consecutive runs captured to files (the first full run at 23:13 had the two wall-clock failures and one at 23:27 the retry race, both fixed above; the `src/profile` suites 32/32 in three consecutive runs); mobile-core and staff-mobile untouched (`git diff main..HEAD` empty for both). Surface: API on `pilates_dev_sim` with fresh fixtures, the worker running (`start:worker`), client Metro on 8083 (another project holds 8081), a seeded library photo (`simctl addmedia`); the committed `profile-photo.yaml` passed at the first attempt on the iPhone 16 Pro (iOS 18.2, run `2026-10-07_231706`) and on the iPad Pro 11 (M4, run `2026-10-07_232731`, driver connected first time with the iPhone shut down): the login subflow clears state and keychain, so the cold-cache case the row names is what the flow exercises: Lucía logs in and the Perfil tab is tapped at once, Perfil renders «LF», Editar perfil, Cambiar foto through the system picker, the photo in the circle with «Se recorta al centro en forma de círculo.» and Quitar foto, the photo in the Perfil card after Volver, Quitar foto and the initials back on both screens; no red box on either device. The failed → loaded transition was also reproduced on the iPad with three scratch flows: Lucía logged in, the API stopped, Perfil opened («No pudimos cargar tu perfil.» with Reintentar), the API restarted, Reintentar tapped: the Perfil card with «LF» rendered, no red box. Test 9's Perfil steps are unchanged. **CI, first run** [37723409641](https://github.com/DaleneyRuben/emma-pilates/actions/runs/37723409641) (mobile chain only, as the paths filter demands: changes 4 s, quality-mobile 1 m 28 s, mobile 2 m 35 s failed): staff-mobile `nutritionist-flows.spec.tsx` «groups upcoming Evaluations by day and cancels a selected one» failed at 03:38 UTC, that is 23:38 in Bolivia, because its «Hoy» Evaluation is fixed at 23:00 and the agenda only offers Cancelar evaluación for a start still ahead; the same wall-clock class as the two booking specs, and a grep found about fifteen app specs and fixtures that build Sessions, Bookings, Evaluations, and civil dates from `Date.now()` or `new Date()` next to a `today` civil date, each of them wrong for some minutes around midnight in Bolivia. Following the user's instruction that no test may depend on the time of day, `35f46617` freezes the clock for both app suites in their Jest config (`fakeTimers` with `enableGlobally`, `now` 2026-10-08 14:00 UTC, 10:00 in Bolivia, and `doNotFake` for every timer, microtask, animation-frame, and performance API, so only `Date` is faked and `waitFor`, TanStack Query, and the navigators keep real timers); the Nutritionist spec was red at 23:40 without it and both suites were green at 23:41 with it, staff-mobile 33 suites / 115 tests and client-mobile 34 / 140, twice each; mobile-core is not frozen (its three `Date.now()` uses are synthetic expiry instants with no civil-date pairing). `84575849` records the frozen clock in Test 8 of the testing guide. Pull request https://github.com/DaleneyRuben/emma-pilates/pull/10, opened for the user to merge (merge commit, never squash). **CI, second run** [37724055358](https://github.com/DaleneyRuben/emma-pilates/actions/runs/37724055358) green with the mobile chain: changes 4 s, quality-mobile 1 m 36 s, mobile 2 m 52 s (the five other jobs skipped by the paths filter). **Done 2026-10-07** (twenty-seventh session). Evidence commits: `5741ea28`, `16970245`, `aaa32d8e`, `62d80617`, `e73efa8c`, `8b5fd341`, `e94647b4`, `739f6255` (MOB-053), `35f46617`, `84575849`, `598f64ca`. **Decisions taken under the blanket approval**: (1) the loading state reaches `useClientPhoto` as `null`, derived inline from `profile.data?.status === 200`, with no new state and no change to the hook's `null` handling (disabled query, `null` return) or to `use-client-photo.ts`; (2) the new specs reuse `profile-test-support.tsx`'s `profile`, `readRoutes`, and `routeFetcher` but not `openProfile`, which waits for the loaded name; a local `openPerfilTab` and the gated fetcher live in `profile-screen.spec.tsx` because no other spec needs them; (3) the failure path renders exactly as before (`QueryFailure` with Reintentar) and the spec drives it with a 500 that keeps failing until the retry; (4) the inline disable and its comment were dropped in the green commit itself because the line they annotated moved, and there is no refactor commit because nothing was left to tidy; (5) the two wall-clock booking specs were fixed at the user's mid-session instruction in their own commit, before the broader cause was found; (6) the retry spec was hardened after one flaky full run instead of being left as a known flake; (7) after CI failed on a third wall-clock spec, the clock was frozen for both app suites in the Jest config (`Date` only, timers real) rather than rewriting fifteen specs one by one, and mobile-core was left on the real clock; (8) the Design column was corrected to Client 18; (9) the testing guide gained one paragraph on the frozen clock in Test 8 and Test 9 is unchanged; (10) MOB-053 opened for an error boundary in the apps, in its own commit. Leftovers not taken: the `act()` warnings the two app suites print and the forced Jest worker exit; the committed `profile-photo.yaml` still needs the worker and a seeded library photo, as Test 9 says. |
| MOB-052   | P3       | Mobile              | Rewrite the eleven React Hooks warnings MOB-050 left: the six `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` «notice after navigation» effects (`bookings-screen.tsx`, `my-evaluations-screen.tsx`, `pending-payments-screen.tsx`, `client-bookings-screen.tsx`, `navigation.tsx`'s roster outcomes, mobile-core `authentication-flow.tsx`), the three `react-hooks/purity` `Date.now()` reads in render (`my-evaluations-screen.tsx`'s cancellable flag, `navigation.tsx`'s post-session flag, `pending-registration-screens.tsx`'s lapsed check), and the two `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` warnings in mobile-core (`authentication-flow.tsx`'s verification effect without `onVerified`, `use-pending-registration.ts`'s once-per-launch restore); then put `set-state-in-effect` and `purity` back to `error` in `packages/tooling/eslint/mobile.js` | MOB-050 | — | done | Opened by MOB-050 on 2026-10-07. The rules are React Compiler rules shipped in `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` 7; the apps do not enable the compiler (`experiments.reactCompiler` unset), so the patterns are not defects today, but each rewrite changes render timing and needs its own red → green and a surface run **Compared with the code 2026-10-08** (twenty-eighth session, branch `chore/mob-052-react-hooks-warnings`). The row was right about the count and the sites: lint from a clean shell prints exactly eleven warnings and no error, mobile-core 4 (`authentication-flow.tsx` 83:6 `exhaustive-deps`, the verification effect without `onVerified`, which MOB-050 cited as line 96 before its Prettier pass; 126:7 `set-state-in-effect`, `setRoute(routeForIntent(intent))`; `pending-registration-screens.tsx` 79:7 `purity`; `use-pending-registration.ts` 40:6 `exhaustive-deps`, `onRestored`, `restore`, and `store` left out of `[]`), client-mobile 3 (`bookings-screen.tsx` 137:5 `set-state-in-effect`; `my-evaluations-screen.tsx` 65:81 `purity` and 117:5 `set-state-in-effect`), staff-mobile 4 (`pending-payments-screen.tsx` 53:5 and `client-bookings-screen.tsx` 144:5 `set-state-in-effect`; `navigation.tsx` 67:5 `set-state-in-effect` and 95:63 `purity`); the two rules sit at `warn` on lines 64–65 of `packages/tooling/eslint/mobile.js`. What the sites do: (1) `BookingsScreen` seeds `noticeFor` from `route.params.confirmed.bookingId`, then the effect copies the same id again whenever `confirmed` changes and nulls it 8 s later; the strip renders while `noticeFor === confirmed.bookingId`, the param is never cleared; `session-detail-screen.tsx` (Reservar) and `reschedule-screen.tsx` (Reprogramar) navigate with `confirmed`; `session-detail.spec.tsx` «shows the Session facts and books…» and `my-bookings.spec.tsx` «reschedules a Booking…» assert «Reserva confirmada», `book-and-cancel-class.yaml` waits for it, nothing covers the fade. (2) `MyEvaluationsScreen` is the same with `confirmed.id`, which also drives the card's Éxito edge (`highlighted`); `book-evaluation-screen.tsx` navigates with the booked `ClientEvaluationDto`; `evaluations.spec.tsx` «lists only days with open slots and books one…» asserts «Evaluación confirmada», `evaluation-book-and-cancel.yaml` too. (3) `PendingPaymentsScreen` starts `notice` at null, the effect copies `route.params.notice` («Pago confirmado» or «Pago rechazado»), clears the param at once with `setParams`, and nulls the state after 2 s; `review-payment-screen.tsx` navigates with it; `administrator-flows.spec.tsx` «reviews a transfer…» and «requires a reason to reject…» assert both notices; the committed `administrator-pending-payments.yaml` reviews nothing. (4) `ClientBookingsScreen` is the same shape with `route.params.confirmed`, 8 s; it is reached through `useShowClientBookings` (a stack reset to a fresh `ClientBookings` route on the phone, `workspace.show` on the tablet, where `client-panel.tsx` renders it with `route={{ params: panel }}` and a `setParams` that rewrites the panel); `assistant-flows.spec.tsx` «books a Session for the Client…» and `assistant-tablet.spec.tsx` «books a Session in a two-column grid…» assert it. (5) `TrainerRosterScreen` (in `navigation.tsx`) keeps `outcomes` in state and the effect replaces the whole map with the roster's saved `attendanceOutcome`s whenever `roster.data` changes, so a refetch (TanStack's `staleTime` is 0, `refetchOnWindowFocus` is on, and the provider maps AppState to `focusManager`) throws away the Trainer's unsaved taps; `trainer-flows.spec.tsx` restores saved outcomes and saves a correction; `trainer-agenda-and-inbox.yaml` opens no roster. (6) `AuthenticationFlow` seeds `route` from the intent and the effect sets it again when `intent` changes (a link received while Login is up, as `VerifiedLinkProvider` delivers them after mount); `authentication-flow.spec.tsx` «opens a verified link received while Login is already visible» and «verifies a Client email link…», and client `app-shell.spec.tsx` «verifies a Client email link at startup» cover it. (7) `UpcomingCard` re-reads `Date.now()` on every render to choose between «Puedes cancelar hasta 3 horas antes.» with Cancelar and «Ya no se puede cancelar» (the server's `cancellable` flag is a snapshot); `evaluations.spec.tsx` «lists upcoming and past Evaluations…» has one card 2 h ahead and one 10 days ahead, under the frozen clock since MOB-051. (8) `isPostSession` (`endsAt <= Date.now()`) decides between the Asistió/No asistió segments with Guardar asistencia and «Podrás marcar asistencia cuando termine la clase.»; the spec's roster ends in 2000. (9) `CheckEmailScreen` compares `Date.now()` with `pending.pendingExpiresAt` on every render to show `RegistrationLapsed`; `pending-registration-screens.spec.tsx` «says the registration lapsed…» and `authentication-flow-pending.spec.tsx` «says the registration lapsed once, then forgets it» cover it (mobile-core keeps the real clock). (10) `VerifyEmailScreen`'s `onVerified` is `() => setPending(null)`, a new closure on every render of the flow: listing it would re-run `verifyClientEmail` with a consumed token on every parent render. (11) the restore in `usePendingRegistration` is once per launch by design; `onRestored` is an inline arrow, `restore` flips when a link arrives. The design prompts say nothing about how a notice reaches its screen or when the clock is read: follow-up 24 wants the strip transient («fades out after a few seconds or on the first scroll, and never appears again for that booking»; the code fades after 8 s and ignores scrolling, unchanged here), follow-up 44 fixes the 3-hour rule and keeps the «Evaluación confirmada» notice, staff 12 wants «a brief toast confirmation» on return; no follow-up is needed. Existing code to reuse: `useCountdown` is the only timer hook, `isInsideCancellationWindow(startsAt, now)` already takes `now` from its caller (the booking cards pass `new Date()`, which the rule does not flag), no clock hook exists, `useRef` is used once (an idempotency key), and `navigation.setParams` is already how two of the four notice screens clear their param. React 19.2.3 ships `useEffectEvent` (typed in `@types/react` 19.2) and `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` 7.1.1 excludes its result from `exhaustive-deps`. A lint probe on a scratch file settled the rewrites: a `useState` initializer reading `Date.now()` passes `purity`, a `useMemo` reading it does not (and `exhaustive-deps` calls its key unnecessary), the React docs' «adjust state when a prop changes» `setState` during render passes, and an effect that only schedules a timer around an effect event passes both rules. **Built 2026-10-08** (twenty-eighth session). Group (a), the six `set-state-in-effect` sites: red `a3c3977f` (five specs for a `useTransientNotice` hook in mobile-core: null without a notice, dismissed exactly when its time is up, the timer kept and the latest `dismiss` called across re-renders, a new notice starting over, the timer cleared on unmount; red for the missing module), `2de31e22` (the spec awaits RNTL 14's async `renderHook`, `rerender`, and `unmount`, and advances the fake timers inside `await act(async …)`: a synchronous `act` around `advanceTimersByTime` leaves every later render's effects unflushed in that file, still red for the missing module), green `33dfde98` (`packages/mobile-core/src/ui/transient-notice.ts`, 24 lines: returns the param or null and schedules one timer around an effect event, exported from the package index), refactor `7ba909d0` (the four screens: `BookingsScreen`, `MyEvaluationsScreen`, `PendingPaymentsScreen`, and `ClientBookingsScreen` read their notice from `route.params` through the hook and clear the param with `navigation.setParams` when the 8 s or 2 s are up; the `noticeFor`/`notice` state, the four effects, and the two eager `setParams` calls are gone; the client navigation types gain `setParams`; the Éxito edge of the Evaluation card follows the notice's `id`; 25 insertions, 45 deletions; the six covering specs 16/16 in each app); red `8ee5332f` (`trainer-flows.spec.tsx` taps Luis's Asistió, flips Ana's saved outcome on the server, refetches through `focusManager.setFocused(false)` then `true`, and expects Ana to follow the server and Luis to keep the tap: Luis was deselected), green `e94d1d9e` (`TrainerRosterScreen` keeps only the Trainer's `edits` in state and computes `outcomes = { ...savedOutcomes(session.bookings), ...edits }` after the early returns; `savedOutcomes` narrows the DTO's `null`/`undefined` outcome without the old cast, which the typecheck demanded; the effect is gone); `9a5711d7` (`AuthenticationFlow` keeps the intent it last saw in state and, when the prop differs, sets both the seen intent and the route during the same render, the React docs' «adjust state when a prop changes»; no red: the behaviour is unchanged and already specified by «opens a verified link received while Login is already visible» and the client `app-shell.spec.tsx` startup link, both green before and after). Group (b), the three `purity` reads: red `62c477a7` (two specs for `useMountedAt`: one clock read kept across re-renders, a new read for a new mount, through `jest.spyOn(Date, "now")`), green `e29dadad` (`packages/mobile-core/src/time/mounted-at.ts`: a `useState` initializer reading `Date.now()`, which the rule accepts, exported from the index), refactor `566fabb8` (`UpcomingCard` compares `cancellableUntil` with the instant the card appeared, `TrainerRosterScreen` compares `endsAt` with the instant the roster opened, `CheckEmailScreen` compares `pendingExpiresAt` with the instant it appeared; the Evaluation, roster, and pending-registration specs 11/11, 12/12, 20/20). Group (c), the two `exhaustive-deps` sites: guard `d085405e` (two characterisation specs, green before and after: «verifies the token once however often the flow re-renders» re-renders the flow twice with a new `notice` and expects one `verifyClientEmail` call, «restores once however often the flow re-renders meanwhile» holds `store.load` open, re-renders the flow, releases it, and expects «Revisa tu correo» and one load; with the rule's own suggestion applied instead, `onVerified` and `[onRestored, restore, store]` listed, three specs fail: the token is verified three times and the restore never ends, so the flow stays blank), `c4f99bcc` (`VerifyEmailScreen` reads `onVerified` through `useEffectEvent` and its effect keeps `[api, token]`, now complete), `ed72160e` (`usePendingRegistration` reads `store`, `restore`, and `onRestored` through one effect event called at the start of a `[]` effect, which then loads, clears a lapsed registration, sets state in its `then`, and announces; a first version that did the whole load inside the async effect event tripped `set-state-in-effect`, so the effect event only returns the props). Then `2a1deae5` deletes the two `warn` overrides and their comment from `packages/tooling/eslint/mobile.js`, so both rules are errors through the recommended set again, and `65f94831` rewrites Test 8's «prints only warnings» paragraph and the root `pnpm lint` note. The lapsed branch itself («El registro venció») cannot be reached on a device without a seven-day wait; its two specs cover it and the same `useMountedAt` read renders the live «Revisa tu correo» seen on both devices. iPad Pro 11 (M4): the committed booking flow failed at «Reserva confirmada» because its default `EMAIL` is Sofía and her fixture Plan has expired on the rebuilt database («No te quedan clases en tu plan.», `Comprar un plan`; the pre-rebuild copy had carried a later Plan for her from an earlier session), so a scratch copy with `EMAIL: lucia@example.com` ran and passed («Te quedan 3 clases en tu plan»); the Evaluation flow, the registration and relaunch (`mob052ipad-…@example.com`), the verification link, Paola's review (Mariana's Bs 560 transfer, created through the API: purchase intent, receipt upload with the base64 SHA-256 checksum, finalize, the worker's `ready`, attach, submit), Camila's booking in the split view (the notice inside the right panel beside Buscar cliente, then gone), and Emma's roster all passed at the first attempt; every screen laid out as on the phone, the split view included. Evidence commits: `4d682010`, `a3c3977f`, `2de31e22`, `33dfde98`, `7ba909d0`, `8ee5332f`, `e94d1d9e`, `9a5711d7`, `62c477a7`, `e29dadad`, `566fabb8`, `d085405e`, `c4f99bcc`, `ed72160e`, `2a1deae5`, `65f94831`, `1168e127`. **Decisions taken under the blanket approval**: (1) each navigation notice reaches its screen as the route param itself and `useTransientNotice` clears the param through `navigation.setParams` when its time is up, so the four screens hold no notice state and the two eager `setParams` calls on arrival are gone; (2) the hook's `dismiss` is an effect event and the hook lives in `packages/mobile-core/src/ui/transient-notice.ts`, exported from the index, because two apps use it; (3) the roster derives `outcomes` from the saved outcomes plus the Trainer's `edits` during render, so a refetch keeps an unsaved tap, the one behaviour change, specified in `trainer-flows.spec.tsx`; (4) `AuthenticationFlow` adjusts its route during render on an intent change, with no red checkpoint because nothing observable changes; (5) «now» is read once per mount through `useMountedAt` (`packages/mobile-core/src/time/mounted-at.ts`) at the three sites, `useMemo` keyed on the data being flagged by the same rule, and the server's flags plus the refetch on mount carry the live truth; (6) `onVerified` stays out of the verification effect's dependencies through `useEffectEvent`; (7) the restore stays once per launch with a `[]` effect reading its props through one effect event, a ref guard with listed dependencies rejected because a link flipping `restore` mid-load would blank the flow, and the effect event returns the props instead of loading because an async effect event that sets state trips `set-state-in-effect`; (8) the two group (c) specs are guards committed before the change, with the rule's suggested fix shown to fail three specs; (9) both rules return to `error` by deleting the overrides, so the recommended set governs; (10) Test 8 gains one paragraph and no other Test changes; (11) the mobile-core hook specs fake timers locally with `await act(async …)`, the apps' frozen clock untouched; (12) the mutated dev database was rebuilt from the migrations between the devices, and Paola's iPad review used a transfer created through the API for Mariana. Leftovers not taken: the `act()` warnings the two app suites print and the forced Jest worker exit; the committed `book-and-cancel-class.yaml` defaults to Sofía, whose fixture Plan is expired, so it passes only on a database that carries a later Plan for her. |
| MOB-053   | P3       | Mobile              | Catch render errors in both apps: neither client-mobile nor staff-mobile nor mobile-core has an error boundary, so a thrown render error (the kind MOB-051 fixed) unmounts the whole tree, a red box in Expo Go and a crash in a release build; wrap the navigators in a boundary that shows a recoverable «Algo salió mal» screen with a retry and reports the error, with a spec that throws from a child | MOB-051 | — | done | Opened by MOB-051 on 2026-10-07 while confirming that nothing catches «Rendered more hooks than during the previous render»; a design prompt may be needed for the recovery screen **Compared with the code 2026-10-08** (twenty-ninth session, branch `feature/mob-053-error-boundary`). The row was right: no error boundary exists in client-mobile, staff-mobile, or mobile-core (a grep for `componentDidCatch`, `getDerivedStateFromError`, `ErrorBoundary`, and `react-error-boundary` over the three packages finds nothing, and no package depends on `react-error-boundary`). Where the roots are composed: `MobileApplication` (`packages/mobile-core/src/application.tsx`) returns null until the fonts load and then nests `MobileQueryProvider` → `VerifiedLinkProvider` → `MobileIdentityProvider` → `IdentityNavigation`, which renders the app's `Navigation` once the identity is no longer restoring; `ClientNavigation` renders `AuthenticatedClientNavigation` (`createStaticNavigation` over the `ClientTabs` bottom-tab navigator and its four native stacks) inside a second `MobileQueryProvider` and the fetcher contexts when the role is `client`, otherwise `AuthenticationFlow` (or `EmailChangeLinkScreen` for a link); `StaffNavigation` picks one of the four `createStaticNavigation` Role stacks (`roleNavigation`) inside `MobileQueryProvider`, `StaffFetcherContext`, and `AssistantWorkspaceProvider`, otherwise `AuthenticationFlow`. What already renders a recoverable failure: `QueryFailure` (`ui/query-failure.tsx`: a centred `BodyText` with `accessibilityRole="alert"` and a `PrimaryButton` «Reintentar», no title) is what the data screens show with per-screen copy («No pudimos cargar tu inicio.», «No pudimos cargar la lista de asistencia.»); its action is the right one but it has no title or explanation, while `EmptyState variant="plain"` (`ui/empty-state.tsx`, MOB-040: a centred Lora 21 title, an optional 14 px muted note up to 260 px, an optional 220 px action, an optional quiet 34 px glyph) carries exactly the three pieces the row asks for, so the recovery screen reuses `Screen` + `EmptyState variant="plain"` + `PrimaryButton` and neither component changes (no glyph fits an error, so none is drawn). React Navigation: both apps are on `@react-navigation/native` 7.3.18 over core 7.21.13 with static navigators; the container lives inside each `createStaticNavigation` component and no state persistence is configured, so a boundary above it that remounts its subtree loses every tab and stack (the tree starts at the first route again); core 7 offers `screenLayout` on a navigator (and `layout` on a screen), a function that wraps each screen's element inside the navigator, which the React Navigation docs name as the place for an error boundary, so a boundary there replaces only the failed screen and keeps the navigator, its state, the tab bar, and the other tabs. How a thrown render error reaches the console today: React 19.2.3's Fabric renderer (`ReactFabric-dev.js`) routes it to the root's `onUncaughtError` when nothing catches it, the root unmounts, and RN's `setUpErrorHandling` hands it to `ExceptionsManager.handleException(e, true)`: the full-screen red box in Expo Go and a crash in a release build; with a boundary the renderer calls `onCaughtError`, which logs through `console.error` («An error occurred in the <X> component… React will try to recreate this component tree from scratch using the error boundary you provided»), and `ExceptionsManager.installConsoleErrorReporter` turns that into a red LogBox notification in development and a console line in release. «Reports the error» without a provider account therefore means `componentDidCatch` writing the error and the component stack through `console.error`; no Sentry, no new dependency. Design: no prompt draws a generic error state («Algo salió mal» and «inesperado» appear nowhere in `docs/design/prompts/` or `design/`; the 40 prompts that mention «error» are field errors, the Error tone, and the `QueryFailure` copies), and the README's «Cards» and «Lists between rules» sections describe only the framed and plain empty states, so follow-up 89 proposes the recovery screen (its states and copy) before it is built. **Built 2026-10-08** (twenty-ninth session). Follow-up 89 (`c620e66d`, `docs/design/prompts/followups/89-recovery-screen.md`, indexed 🕓 pending and listed under «Pending design exports») proposes the recovery screen as the plain empty state with an action: «Algo salió mal» (Lora 21), the note «No pudimos mostrar esta pantalla. Inténtalo de nuevo; si vuelve a pasar, cierra la app y ábrela otra vez.» (14 px muted, 260 px), and the regular «Reintentar» primary button, no card, no icon, one «Pantalla de recuperación» card in the design system and a README section; the apps are built from that copy and the export is design accuracy only. | MOB-054   | P3       | Mobile              | Remove the `act()` warnings the two app Jest suites print and the open handle that makes every mobile suite need `--forceExit`: find the handle with `--detectOpenHandles` (TanStack's persister, the AppState and NetInfo subscriptions, and the navigators are the suspects); spec files, `jest.setup.ts`, and test support only, no behaviour change | MOB-053 | — | done | Opened by MOB-053 on 2026-10-08 (the row itself is added by pull request 13; this branch carries a copy so its evidence has a home, and the two copies conflict on this one line when the second of the two pull requests merges: keep this line). **Compared with the code 2026-10-08** (twenty-ninth session, branch `chore/mob-054-mobile-jest-act-warnings`, from `main` with pull requests 12 and 13 still open). The row is half right. The warnings are real: a clean-shell `jest --ci` run of `main` prints 383 `console.error` blocks in client-mobile and 53 in staff-mobile, of three kinds: «An update to <X> inside a test was not wrapped in act(...)» (133 / 27; `HomeScreen` 35, `BrowseSessionsScreen` 26, `ProfileScreen` 16, `ProfileTabButton` 14, `BottomTabView` 10… in the Client app, `AccountAvatar` 8, `TrainerAgendaScreen` 6, `StaffProfileScreen` 6… in the Staff app), every one of them thrown from TanStack's `notifyManager` batch timer (`notifyManager.ts:42`, a `setTimeout` that delivers query results to their observers after a spec's last assertion); «The current testing environment is not configured to support act(...)» (247 / 26), thrown from React Navigation's `PreventRemoveProvider` effect and from the same TanStack timer while the act environment is off but an act scope is still open; and no «overlapping act» yet. The causes are in the specs, not in the app: RNTL 14 made `render`, `fireEvent`, `cleanup`, `rerender`, and `unmount` asynchronous (each is an `act(async …)` that flips `IS_REACT_ACT_ENVIRONMENT` on and restores it when its promise settles), and 61 statement-level `fireEvent.*` calls in 14 spec files (`purchase-flow` 11, `profile-screen` 10, `client-shortcuts` 7, `staff-profile` 6, `session-detail` 6, `trainer-flows` 5…) plus one in `profile-test-support.tsx`'s `openProfile`, and 52 `cleanup()` calls in `afterEach` blocks of 50 files, are not awaited, so their act scope is still open when the next `waitFor` turns the environment off to poll: React then reports every update that lands in that window. RNTL 14 never sets `IS_REACT_ACT_ENVIRONMENT` globally (its `act.js` sets it to true inside its own `act` and restores the previous value, `undefined`), so an update between two awaited calls is reported as «not configured» rather than flushed. The open handle is not real: on `main`, `jest --ci` without `--forceExit` exits on its own in mobile-core (4.5 s after start, 226 tests), client-mobile (7.0 s, 140), and staff-mobile (12.2 s, 116), and three scratch specs (empty, an import of `Text`, a rendered `Text`) exit at once; `--detectOpenHandles` on `app-shell.spec.tsx` ran for five minutes without naming a handle because there is none to name. The line «Force exiting Jest: Have you considered using `--detectOpenHandles`» that every run shows is printed by Jest whenever `--forceExit` is passed, whatever the handles; the 2026-09-22 note that staff-mobile «hangs after the summary» predates the NetInfo and AsyncStorage mocks in `jest.setup.ts` and no longer reproduces. The suspects the row names (TanStack's persister, the AppState and NetInfo subscriptions, the navigators) are all cleaned up by `MobileQueryProvider`'s effect or mocked in the setup. `--forceExit` therefore stays only where it is harmless (the CI workflow and the testing guide pass it; dropping it is a one-line chore outside this row's «spec files, `jest.setup.ts`, and test support» scope and is left as a leftover). TanStack's own test setup routes `notifyManager` through `act` (`setNotifyFunction`); React's testing docs ask every test environment to set `IS_REACT_ACT_ENVIRONMENT`. **Built 2026-10-08** (twenty-ninth session). Red `7581a050` (both apps' `jest.setup.ts` collect every «not wrapped in act», «not configured to support act», and «overlapping act» line that reaches `console.error`, still printing it, and `afterEach` and `afterAll` expect the list to be empty: with the suites as they were, 7 of 9 specs in `subscription-screen.spec.tsx` plus `unread-badges.spec.tsx` and 2 of 2 in `trainer-flows.spec.tsx` fail on the guard). Green `30eb057a` (`await` in front of the 61 statement-level `fireEvent.*` calls in 14 spec files and `profile-test-support.tsx`'s `openProfile`, and in front of the 52 `cleanup()` calls, whose `afterEach`/`afterAll` blocks become `async`; 51 files, 166 lines changed, no assertion touched; still red on TanStack's timer alone) and `52e4c441` (both setups set `IS_REACT_ACT_ENVIRONMENT = true` and give TanStack's `notifyManager` a synchronous scheduler so a query result reaches its observers inside the awaited `render`, `fireEvent`, or `waitFor` that produced it; a first attempt that instead routed every batch through React's `act` while the environment was on reached 0 «not wrapped» and 0 «not configured» in the Staff app but left 69 «not configured» in the Client app, 3 «overlapping act», and two Staff specs red (`ReviewPaymentScreen`'s receipt and the Assistant search delivered inside an outer act), so it was replaced). `5194584d` adds the guard, the rule for fixing a failure, and the exit without `--forceExit` to Test 8. | MOB-056   | P3       | Mobile identity     | A verification link that arrives while a kept pending registration is still loading ends on «Revisa tu correo» instead of the link's screen, because the restore's `onRestored` sets the route after the intent did (`packages/mobile-core/src/auth/authentication-flow.tsx`; the guard spec «restores once however often the flow re-renders meanwhile» in `authentication-flow-pending.spec.tsx` is the starting point); decide the rule (the link's screen wins; the kept registration stays stored) and record it | MOB-052 | Client 01, 20 | done | Opened by MOB-053 on 2026-10-08 (the row itself is added by pull request 13; this branch carries a copy so its evidence has a home, and the two copies conflict on this one line when the second of the two pull requests merges: keep this line). **Compared with the code 2026-10-08** (twenty-ninth session, branch `fix/mob-056-link-during-restore`, from `main` with pull requests 12, 13, and 14 still open). The row is right. `AuthenticationFlow` seeds `route` from the intent it mounts with and, since MOB-052 (`9a5711d7`), adjusts it during render when the `intent` prop changes («state adjusted when a prop changes»); `usePendingRegistration` starts the once-per-launch restore at mount when the flow mounts without a link (`restore: intent === null || intent === undefined`), renders nothing while `restoring`, and when `store.load` settles with a kept registration sets it and calls the `onRestored` it captured at mount, which is `() => setRoute("check-email")` unconditionally. The Client app mounts the flow with no intent at a cold start and `VerifiedLinkProvider` delivers the startup link a moment later (`getInitialURL` is asynchronous), so the order «mount → restore starts → link arrives → route = verify-email → load settles → route = check-email» is the normal cold-start order on a device whose Client kept a registration and then tapped the email's link: the link's screen is overwritten by «Revisa tu correo», the token is never verified, and the Client is told to check the email she just came from. `VerifyEmailScreen` verifies on mount, so the overwrite also happens before the request is sent (the flow renders null while restoring). The two guard specs of MOB-052 («verifies the token once…», «restores once however often the flow re-renders meanwhile») hold `store.load` open and re-render the flow, but neither delivers an intent during the load. Rule to take: **the link's screen wins** (a link is a deliberate act by the person; a kept registration is a convenience that is still there afterwards), and **the kept registration stays stored and loaded**, so «Revisa tu correo» is reachable again on the next launch without a link, and the lapsed-registration cleanup still runs. The narrowest change is in the flow, not the hook: `onRestored` moves to «check-email» only while the route is still `login`, through a functional `setRoute`, so a route a link chose meanwhile is kept; the hook's once-per-launch effect, its effect event, and its `setPendingState(kept)` are untouched. **Built 2026-10-08** (twenty-ninth session). Red `27f1f1ed` (`authentication-flow-pending.spec.tsx`: a spec holds `store.load` open with a kept registration, re-renders the flow with a `verify-email` intent, releases the load, and expects «Correo verificado», the token verified, and no «Revisa tu correo»; red: «Revisa tu correo» was on screen and the token never verified), `7032a704` (the spec now expects the verified email to complete the kept registration as the existing rule says, `store.current()` null, and a second spec answers the link with `invalid`, expects «Enlace no válido», no `store.clear`, and the registration still stored; both red without the fix, 2 of 8 in the file), green `be75eb63` (`AuthenticationFlow`'s `onRestored` is `setRoute((current) => (current === "login" ? "check-email" : current))`: the restore moves to «Revisa tu correo» only while the route is still Login, so a route a link chose meanwhile stays; one line and a comment, `use-pending-registration.ts` untouched; the 18 `src/auth` suites 108/108). `f4b3303e` adds the rule to Test 5 of the testing guide. No API, contract, migration, dependency, or `design/` change; no `eslint-disable`; every touched file under 400 lines (`authentication-flow.tsx` 242). Verified from a clean shell (`env -u DATABASE_URL -u NODE_OPTIONS -u TEST_DATABASE_URL`, Node 24.13.0): lint exit 0 with 0 errors and 0 warnings in mobile-core, client-mobile, and staff-mobile, `format:check` and typecheck clean in all three, Jest `--ci --forceExit` captured to files and run twice each: mobile-core 52 suites / 228 tests (226 + 2), client-mobile 34 / 140, staff-mobile 33 / 116 (the first pair of client-mobile runs, taken while the machine's load average was above 130 from other projects' processes, had 1 and then 33 timeouts of 20 s; two runs taken afterwards with the machine quiet are the clean pair cited, and the branch does not touch client-mobile). No simulator run: Expo Go cannot receive the link (LNK-001), the decision is a flow rule covered by the two specs, and no screen changes its drawing; the surface was not exercised and nothing here implies it was. Evidence commits: `81d0917f`, `27f1f1ed`, `7032a704`, `be75eb63`, `f4b3303e`, `847e4e8b`, `f012ad01`. **Decisions taken under the blanket approval**: (1) the link's screen wins, because a link is a deliberate act by the person and the kept registration is a convenience that is still there afterwards; (2) the kept registration stays stored and loaded through the link: a verified email completes and forgets it as the existing rule says, an invalid link leaves it stored, so the next launch without a link opens «Revisa tu correo» again; (3) the change sits in the flow's `onRestored` (the route moves only from Login), not in `usePendingRegistration`, whose once-per-launch effect and effect event are untouched; (4) after an invalid link, «Ir a iniciar sesión» opens Login rather than «Revisa tu correo», and the kept registration shows at the next launch; (5) the row's copy on this branch conflicts with pull request 13's on one line, resolved by keeping this line. Leftovers not taken: none beyond the rows pull request that opens the item. |

Reproduced from a clean shell (`env -u DATABASE_URL -u NODE_OPTIONS -u TEST_DATABASE_URL`): `pnpm --filter @pilates/backend test:e2e -- test/app.e2e-spec.ts` → 8/8 failed, `error: role "test" does not exist` thrown by `boss.start()` in `startInstalledPgBoss`, called from the `registrationPgBoss` factory (`system-identity.module.ts:139`; the `uploadsPgBoss` factory in `uploads.module.ts:84` has the same shape), because `test/setup-environment.ts` sets `DATABASE_URL ??= postgresql://test:test@localhost:5432/test`, a URL that does reach the local server; `pnpm exec jest src/jobs/pg-boss-runtime` → 1/5 failed, `TypeError: A dynamic import callback was invoked without --experimental-vm-modules` at `pg-boss-runtime.ts:36`. What the row misses: (1) the dynamic import is the only place that needs the flag: `schedule-authentication-email.ts` and `schedule-finalize-upload.ts` already import `fromPrisma` statically, which TypeScript 5.9 under `module: nodenext` compiles to `require("pg-boss")` (see `dist/src/jobs/authentication-email/schedule-authentication-email.js`), so the API and worker have relied on Node 24's `require(esm)` since BE-004, and the dynamic import came with `277ff8ed` (2026-08-30) with no recorded reason; (2) the e2e and integration Jest configs are ESM (`extensionsToTreatAsEsm`, `useESM`), so those two suites need the flag by Jest's design whatever pg-boss does, and only the unit config (in `package.json`) can become flag-free; (3) the real-database boot of `AppModule` is already covered by `client-registration-runtime.integration-spec.ts` (a disposable database through `startDatabaseTestContext`, real pg-boss through the module factories), so a disposable database in the e2e spec would duplicate it; (4) both modules already have an offline boot mode (`OPENAPI_GENERATION=true`, `isOpenApiGeneration`) that returns a stub pg-boss for the OpenAPI generator; (5) `startDatabaseTestContext` without `TEST_DATABASE_URL` needs Docker (Testcontainers), not running here, so the «disposable database» option would still need operator-set environment on a Docker-less machine. Every place that sets or assumes the flag or the URL — the flag: `apps/backend/package.json` `test`, `test:watch`, `test:cov`, `test:e2e`, `test:integration` (not `test:debug`, and not the root `check:api-compat`, which runs a bare `pnpm --filter @pilates/backend exec jest`), `docs/testing-guide.md` Test 8 «Backend unit tests» (the ExperimentalWarning paragraph), the readiness checklist line, and the memory note «Backend local test caveats»; no runbook mentions it and there is no CI workflow. Runtime wiring smoke on `pilates_test_be014` (copy of `pilates_dev_sim`, `node dist/src/main.js` on 3100 after `nest build`, whose `pg-boss-runtime.js` now reads `require("pg-boss")`): `GET /api/v1/health` 404 `resource_not_found` Problem, `/health/live` 200, `/health/ready` 200 `ready`, `POST /api/v1/client-registrations` 202 with a `system.authentication-email.v1` job row created through the statically loaded pg-boss; API stopped, database dropped. No migration, no OpenAPI change. Decisions taken under the blanket approval: (1) the e2e suite boots without a database rather than through `startDatabaseTestContext`, because the suite is documented as an HTTP contract check, a disposable database would need Docker or `TEST_DATABASE_URL` (operator-set environment, the thing this item removes), and `client-registration-runtime.integration-spec.ts` already boots the real `AppModule` on a disposable database; (2) the existing `OPENAPI_GENERATION` gate is reused instead of exporting the two private pg-boss tokens for `overrideProvider` or adding a new env knob, so module internals stay private and no production code changes for a test; renaming the gate to say «boot without the job runtime» was left as a leftover because it would touch the generator and `check:api` for a name; (3) the placeholder `DATABASE_URL` is assigned, not defaulted, so a shell export can never route an e2e run to a real database, and it points at port 1 so any future boot-time connection fails fast with `ECONNREFUSED`; (4) the dynamic import became a static one instead of moving the flag, because TypeScript 5.9 under `nodenext` already compiles the schedule modules' static pg-boss imports to `require`, the API and worker have run on Node 24's `require(esm)` since BE-004, and no runner (editors, `test:debug`, the root `check:api-compat`, a future CI job) then needs the flag for the unit suite; (5) the unit suite's double is a Jest manual node-module mock in `src/__mocks__` (used automatically, documented by Jest for exactly this) rather than a `jest.mock` factory in every spec that reaches `worker.ts` or `pg-boss-runtime.ts`, with the schedule specs keeping their explicit `fromPrisma` factories; (6) `createPgBoss` became synchronous rather than keeping a needless `async` (lint's `require-await`). Leftovers, not taken: the gate rename above; four `pilates_test_<hex>` databases left on the local server by earlier interrupted integration runs (`0d8fe0d94fb4`, `3da52e3326cb`, `7384a2a4d627`, `73da0421e248`), not dropped because they are not this session's; the 38 pre-existing lint errors. |

| BE-016  | P2       | Backend              | Rename the `OPENAPI_GENERATION` boot gate (`isOpenApiGeneration`) to say what it does, «boot without the job runtime», since the e2e suite now uses it as well as the offline OpenAPI generator; update `generate-openapi.ts`, `test/setup-environment.ts`, both module factories, and the testing guide | BE-014                          | done    | BE-014 leftover (decision 2 in its row); touches `check:api`'s generator, so rerun `pnpm check:api` **Compared with the code 2026-10-07** (twenty-fourth session) with `git grep` over the whole repository (excluding `design/`): the row is right on the four code sites and the guide: `src/openapi/generate-openapi.ts:7` and `test/setup-environment.ts:8` set `OPENAPI_GENERATION = 'true'`, the `registrationPgBoss` factory (`system-identity.module.ts:135`) and the `uploadsPgBoss` factory (`uploads.module.ts:81`) read it through `isOpenApiGeneration(process.env)`, and `docs/testing-guide.md` Test 8 «Backend HTTP end-to-end tests» documents it. Decisions taken under the blanket approval: (1) the new variable is `BOOT_WITHOUT_JOB_RUNTIME=true`, read by `bootsWithoutJobRuntime`, and the stub the factories return is `offlinePgBoss`; (2) the old name is rejected outright, with no transition, because nothing outside the backend's own generator and e2e setup ever set it (no CI step, deployment, or runbook), so a transition would only keep a dead name alive; the spec asserts `OPENAPI_GENERATION=true` has no effect; (3) the gate lives in `src/jobs/boot-without-job-runtime.ts`, beside `pg-boss-runtime.ts`, instead of `src/openapi/`, because it gates the job runtime and has two users; (4) the variable stays out of the environment schema: the factories keep reading `process.env` directly, so behaviour is identical, and a schema entry would present a test and tooling switch as deployment configuration; (5) the gate takes `Readonly<Record<string, string | undefined>>` (the shape of `process.env`) instead of a one-key object, so the spec can assert the retired name is ignored without a cast; (6) the red checkpoint edited the existing spec in place (moved in the refactor) so it failed on its assertions rather than on a missing module; (7) the two module-private stubs stay one per module, as before; extracting them is not part of a rename.
